The current tree is materially safer than v954 through transaction/revision ownership, cancellable lifecycle waits, atomic save/load validation, scoped animation cleanup, texture recovery, and broader archetype tests, but it changes 29 runtime files plus 18 test files and is not yet physically proven as one exact build. Release blockers remain: `SETTINGS_DEVELOPER_TOOLS_ENABLED` is still `true`; no exact post-fix iPhone matrix/30–60 minute soak exists; no final Release Archive/App Store validation has been completed; 66 grandfathered `@ts-nocheck` source files, 57/150 test files containing source-text contracts, and substantial legacy empty-catch/logging debt limit proof strength. Honest readiness excluding assets: saved v954 approximately **63%**, current source approximately **81%**, deterministic/code readiness approximately **93%**. Do not call the app App Store-ready until Developer tools are disabled and the exact Release candidate passes physical and Archive gates.
- The 2026-08-20 post-install `iPhone 13 blue` console capture isolated the user's remaining ordinary-stack rejection. The installed phone bundle predates the capture-driven source fixes: current source now blocks scheduled endgame observation while any immutable special transaction owns the temporary merge-6 destination, serializes every external merge behind the accepted regular stack's 80ms handoff, and permits only stable ordinary sum-below-six stacks during the Magnet/Bottle/Honey visual tail while queuing any newly earned wild until release. A newer accepted stack increments the board revision and cancels the old Magnet post-commit tail before it can respawn, unlock, flag, or evaluate endgame against the newer board. - A physically observed successful-board handoff showed the final ghost grid popping out correctly, then being recreated and popped out a second time immediately before the completion/thumbs-up surface. Root cause was split ownership: `__ccFinalResidualPopOutPrepared` represented only completion, not an in-flight handoff, so the merge resolver and a delayed central endgame callback could concurrently run the same reveal/pop-out; the marker was then cleared before the completion flow, allowing a later generic board exit to resurrect every ghost. `FinalResidualHandoffOwner` now provides one generation-scoped single-flight Promise shared by duplicate Journey/Arcade callers, with searchable `[CC_FINAL_RESIDUAL_OWNER]` start/join/complete diagnostics. The completed marker remains owned until the next `startLevel`/transient reset, and generic board exit explicitly keeps the background/ghost layer hidden and excludes ghost targets once the final residual handoff has completed. Focused lifecycle coverage passes 4 suites / 23 tests; `type-check` passes; `qa:fast` passes every selected gate with **38 suites / 365 tests**. Source only; `dist`, native `Web.bundle`, signed app, and phone were not changed. Deterministic verdict: **PASS**; exact success → single pop-out → thumbs-up cadence remains **NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST**.

- Bottle performance audit on the installed 63-bubble build found a real physical-device frame-budget regression at the Bottle/Magnet handoff: the captured 332ms touch/release window rendered 11 ticker frames, every frame exceeded 20ms, two exceeded 34ms, and the worst reached 45ms (about 33 FPS over that window). Nearby regular drags generally had only one frame over 20ms and 19-32ms maxima. Source accounting shows one Bottle finale owns 13 PNG sprites, 13 wrappers, 63 DOM bubbles, 13 main debris timelines, 3 paper-wave timelines, and 63 bubble timelines before the S.O.S. letter and Magnet effects are counted. The 63 bubbles animate paint-heavy `background-color`, complex `border-radius`, inset `box-shadow`, and transforms every frame; 21 bubble-pop haptic bridge calls are also scheduled. Cleanup is idempotent and tracked, focused lifecycle tests pass 12/12, and no persistent GSAP/Pixi teardown error or retained-owner leak was observed. However, the mixed-tag singleton DOM pool drops wrong-tag nodes on reuse, causing roughly 26 fresh DOM allocations/GC candidates per repeated Bottle finale; this is allocation churn rather than an unbounded leak. Safari/WKWebView did not expose JS heap figures and Instruments could not attach over the current Wi-Fi-only device connection, so absolute MB growth remains unmeasured. No source, bundle, gameplay, or phone build was changed by this audit. Verdict: **FAIL** for the current Bottle peak frame budget; memory-leak verdict remains **NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST** with cable/Instruments or a narrow native telemetry build.

- Latest Journey flip correction: Close is removed immediately when the automatic stats-to-artwork turn starts, and a persistent `data-face="front"` visual guard prevents WebKit from repainting the separately composited Close child in the final artwork frame. Both interactive artwork-to-stats turns and the initial spatial entry explicitly own `is-flipping-to-back`, exempting that guard only during the turn; Close remains physically nested/backface-hidden and therefore becomes visible at the same physical 90deg/50% edge as the stats face instead of waiting for settle. Web-only; native bundle and iPhone remain untouched.
- Journey flip backdrop timing now starts its `520ms` fade-in in the first visible frame immediately after card tap/overlay mount, independently of rotor progress. Close, Play, and dismiss add the backdrop-exit state in the first closing frame so its `520ms` fade-out runs concurrently with the card return instead of waiting for that return to finish. Web-only; native bundle and iPhone remain untouched.
- The Journey flip back face restores the preceding detail modal's accepted internal enter rather than revealing static content. At the physical artwork-to-stats edge, the shared CTA retains its normal `340ms back.out(1.8)` enter while both stat rows and their divider reuse the prior `detailStatPopOut` animation in reverse at the requested 2× speed: `200ms` each with `25ms` DOM-order stagger. Returning to artwork primes those nested elements hidden only after the back face is no longer visible, so every later stats reveal replays cleanly without affecting rotor, card geometry, gyro, or Close ownership. Web-only; native bundle and iPhone remain untouched.
- The two-sided Journey flip now restores the preceding modal's shared `6.8s` idle float on a dedicated owner outside rotor and gyro, so both artwork and stats faces inherit the same gentle idle without transform collisions; input/flip/close pause it and stable settle resumes it. Every overlay opening also restores the prior randomized opposing tilt profile: artwork samples `4.75–6.25deg` left/right and stats samples `2–3.25deg` in the opposite direction. Reduced Motion suppresses idle while retaining the static composition. Web-only; native bundle and iPhone remain untouched.
- The Journey flip idle coach now alternates after each five-second quiet window. The first cycle shows only `DRAG TO FLIP` with the existing hand swipe and rotor preview; the next shows only `TAP TO FLIP`, with the hand pressing once at centre and the complete card making one contained `0.965 → 1.06 → 0.988 → 1` scale bump. The modes continue alternating, share the existing `2.1s` coach lifecycle, stop immediately on input/flip/close, and remain disabled under Reduced Motion. Web-only; native bundle and iPhone remain untouched.
- Random Journey flip tilt no longer waits for landing/settle. The existing `is-flipping-to-back` / `is-flipping-to-front` direction owners select the destination face's sampled tilt in the first enter/flip frame, and the dedicated idle shell transitions toward it throughout the motion so landing arrives already aligned without a final degree correction. Web-only; native bundle and iPhone remain untouched.
- Journey flip exit removes artwork/paper/pseudo shadows in the first `is-exiting` frame, but the white stats surface itself retains full opacity and leaves exclusively through the physical flip-back; the experimental `320ms` stats fade was reverted per visual direction. The Close flip-back now begins 30 timeline percentage points earlier (`2%` instead of `32%`) and reaches its physical edge at `20%`, while entry timing is unchanged. Before stopping live motion, exit samples the current shared idle matrix plus gyro `translate/transform`, disposes their live owners in the same task, and WAAPI-neutralizes the frozen poses to identity over `260ms`; the random overlay tilt concurrently transitions to exact `0deg`. The return therefore reaches the resident Unit with no idle, gyro, or extra-rotation delta. Reduced Motion uses immediate identity. Web-only; native bundle and iPhone remain untouched.
- Direct Journey overlay dismiss now resumes World/Unit idle from the wrapper's exact live landing transform. The resume path passes `preserveInitialTransform` into a `1.8s` idle ramp, preventing `restoreJourneyBoardCardBaseTransform()` from writing the returned Unit to base Y for one frame before idle takes over; the ticker then eases continuously from the captured landing pose. Other fresh Journey idle starts retain their canonical base reset. Web-only; native bundle and iPhone remain untouched.
- Board-to-Journey return is now strictly serialized: the normal remembered-World Journey enter runs first, the overlay return gate accepts only `journeyV700Phase === 'idle'` with both screen and target Unit at `>=0.99` opacity for two consecutive paint frames, and only then the last-played Stage card opens automatically at full initial opacity. The former overlap that allowed card entry during World `entering` at `0.04/0.08` opacity is removed. Web-only; native bundle and iPhone remain untouched.
- Current online checkpoint: `polish Journey card gestures and per-world progression`, commit `91610bc` on `origin/feature/journey-card-flip-prototype`, with unchanged web package `2.0.634`.
- Rollback boundary: `v815 automate QA and finish Journey motion guards` is the last online checkpoint **without 3D Motion**. `v900` is the first consolidated online checkpoint that includes the shared gyro/3D system across Journey, Homepage, and gameplay cubes.
- Checkpoint `v901` consolidates all work after `v900`. Always run `git status --short` before editing and preserve any newer user work.

A stationary tap on either the foreground or rear card now only restarts a scoped 240ms gentle boing on the existing flip/idle owner (`1 → 1.045 → .99 → 1`); it does not change `cardFront`, z-order, modal state, or focus. Both card and modal therefore switch only after the established committed drag/flick threshold. Drag-generated synthetic clicks remain suppressed, and enter/swap/close states reject the tap effect. The timer/class has complete cleanup and Reduced Motion continues to disable the animation. - Fixed the invisible recurring `Swipe to switch` copy. Its per-letter lifecycle was previously attached while the coach layer was hidden, so the complete enter/hold/exit ran during the initial five-second idle delay and left every letter at its terminal zero-scale state before presentation. Letters are now statically primed hidden and acquire the board-game splash animation only from `.is-swipe-coach-active`, guaranteeing a fresh restart on every recurring coach cycle. The requested two-tone addon is explicit: `SWIPE` uses canonical orange `#E8744A` with its dark-orange shadow, while `TO SWITCH` is white with the gameplay neutral shadow. Sequential elastic enter, readable hold, anticipation, staggered scale exit, bottom position, random gesture, hand, and real surface stretching remain unchanged. The same live card still reverse-FLIPs for 620ms to the exact moving Unit geometry and is reinserted on the terminal frame, but a successful Play landing immediately continues into a 320ms local card bounce-out (`1 → 1.08 → 0`, `back.in`-style easing) on the real card inside its cjelina. Opacity stays full through 92% of the shrink, then reaches zero; the card is explicitly left `visibility:hidden`, `opacity:0`, and `scale(0)` until the already-running Journey/World cleanup, so it cannot sit visibly on the Unit after landing. X/backdrop dismiss keeps the existing visible card restoration. The Web Animation owner is tracked, cancellable, releases its fill effect after baking the hidden terminal styles, and safe fallback/Reduced Motion paths hide immediately. X and CTA remain normal controls, backdrop dismissal remains scoped to the true outside stage, and the rear modal keeps its complete geometric drag hit area with a grab cursor. The Unit wrapper continues its normal enter while that same card departs immediately into the 580ms spatial flight. The portal inherits the card's current effective World×viewport opacity and ramps it to one during the first 38% of flight, preventing a low-opacity Unit card from flashing fully opaque when reparented. On committed release/flick, the choreography preserves that ownership: the dragged rear surface continues in the hand's direction and becomes foreground while the former foreground counters behind it. Landing no longer hard-cuts between unlike surfaces: preview and real Unit card share a 160ms smoothstep crossfade while live position tracking continues, both use the exact 12px destination radius and `contain` image fit, and cleanup occurs one aligned frame after the real card reaches full opacity. The transition cancels safely on navigation/pagehide/replacement and never affects legacy detail-origin or interim routes. No build, native sync, or phone install has been performed.

Card and modal share the exact same responsive width (`100vw - 48px`, capped at `390px`), and each active surface independently centers from its own height at the exact viewport midpoint (`top:50%` plus `-50%` self-translation). The supplied Journey → Homepage → Arcade reproduction exposed that `UIManager.hideNavigation()` queried a nonexistent semantic `<nav>` even though the real tree is `<div id="independent-nav">`; hiding therefore depended on MutationObserver timing and allowed the three icon buttons to remain over the Pixi board. `navigation-control.ts` now provides one synchronous ownership switch for the complete tree, `app-zone-manager` releases it before any gameplay/Journey cleanup and acquires it only for Homepage, and `UIManager.showNavigation()` rejects calls outside the `home` zone. An inactive `data-homepage-owner` CSS invariant uses `!important` display/visibility/input isolation, so stale enter/finalize inline writes cannot paint even one gameplay frame; descendant Web Animations are cancelled on release. The first startup follow-up was insufficient because the legacy inline boot CSS still applied `visibility:hidden` to every `#independent-nav` after enter removed its temporary inline override, while the empty initial `#app` DOM node could also be misclassified as active gameplay. Boot CSS is now owner-scoped with `:not([data-homepage-owner='active'])`, and the DOM gameplay fallback is ignored while the authoritative app zone is `home`. This restores cold-start navigation without weakening Journey/gameplay isolation. Native Web.bundle/app/phone were not changed; fresh web cold launch plus Journey→Homepage→Arcade remain the acceptance paths.
- Mushroom spores now occupy five explicit depth bands with approximately 20% of the 72 pooled Graphics in each: foreground `z=140`, behind Mushroom row 1 at `z=88`, behind row 2 at `z=68`, behind row 3 at `z=49`, and behind the rear row at `z=30`. Rear bands add `105ms` progressive birth delay so they visibly begin after the foreground wave. Every spore still rises from below the viewport at `25–32%` screen height/sec with independent wild sway and continuous Honey-highlight shimmer, but its endpoint is now independently randomized across a full 20%-screen band (`y=50–70%`). The prior shared last-cap exit owner is removed: each spore detects its own arrival, flashes once near `24%` of a randomized `300–520ms` terminal window, then pulses/fades to zero and becomes invisible individually. Only after all individual arrivals finish are the shared rise tween and pooled Graphics released outside GSAP render. This reduces the effect from two owners to one and removes synchronized top disappearance, gravity, and all stale exit state. No ticker, listener, texture, or unbounded emitter was added. Native Web.bundle/app/phone were not changed; final depth illusion/arrival rhythm remains `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST` on fresh web replay.
- Mushroom pollen is now a dense bounded spore field rather than 36 similarly large grains. It uses 72 pooled Graphics particles: 72% are fine `0.85–2.2px` spores and the remainder are `2.25–4px`, rendered as narrow grains, dots, vertical spores, or tiny two-speck clusters. Their birth zone spans `1–99%` screen width and `66–97%` height so they cover the complete lower Mushroom pile instead of drifting sparsely across unrelated upper board space. Each retains the visible single-owner `55–85%` alpha and z-index 140, but adds independent vertical flutter to its existing three-segment lateral S-wind path; emission tightens to `22ms` intervals so many different sizes coexist on screen. The effect remains hard-bounded at 72 Graphics plus 30 Mushroom Sprites (102 active objects, below the existing 150-Graphics pool ceiling), owns no ticker/new texture, and defers pool release beyond GSAP render. Focused tests pass and `qa:fast` passes 16 suites / 189 tests plus TypeScript/lint/diff/visual contracts; the no-native-sync production web build passes. Native Web.bundle/app/phone were not changed; final spore density/feel remain `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST` on fresh web replay.
- Mushroom pile timing is lightly accelerated without changing gameplay ownership: birth stagger moves `82→70ms` and reverse exit stagger `58→50ms` (roughly 15% faster), while individual boing shapes remain unchanged. The previously invisible floating pollen had two concrete composition bugs: its fill and display object both used the same translucent alpha, effectively squaring `38–72%` into a much fainter result, and its `zIndex=100` could sort behind the pile's front depth `101–102`. Pollen now draws opaque geometry with one `55–85%` display alpha, owns `zIndex=140`, and follows a three-segment S-shaped wind path with alternating lateral bends instead of one straight drift. Its pool release is also deferred beyond the GSAP render frame through the tracked lifecycle, matching the Mushroom Sprite crash fix. Count, size, palette, Graphics pool, no-ticker design, and cleanup remain bounded. Focused Mushroom/Beach Ball/registry tests, TypeScript, diff hygiene, `qa:fast`, and the no-native-sync production web build pass. Native Web.bundle/app/phone were not changed; final speed/pollen visibility remain `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST` on fresh web replay.
- Mushroom density is retuned after the first enlarged visual replay still exposed board gaps. Inspection confirmed all five sources are transparent 1024×1024 canvases, so overlap based only on canvas bounds was insufficient. The same bounded 30-Sprite pool is now arranged as four extremely interlocked front-to-back rows (`8+8+7+7`) with centers only about 15–16% of screen width apart, while display size is tightened from `135–200px` to `160–200px`. The former tall apex (`y=0.685`) is removed; the shallow top contour settles at `y=0.895–0.945`, keeping the visible pile around the lower 30% rather than approaching mid-screen. Rotation is now independently randomized to `8–15°` in either direction per birth. Existing safe post-GSAP repool boundary, fixed Sprite count, five textures, reverse exit, pollen, and cleanup are unchanged. `qa:fast` passes 16 suites / 189 tests plus TypeScript/lint/diff/visual contracts, and the no-native-sync production web build passes. Native Web.bundle/app/phone were not changed; actual alpha-overlap density remains `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST` on a fresh web replay.
- Mushroom's first enlarged-pile web replay exposed a high-severity GSAP/Pixi lifecycle crash: repeated `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'x')` came from a pooled Pixi target being reset/destroyed during GSAP's own render stack. The visual completion callback previously killed/repooled its Sprite from inside one track while a sibling `bubble.scale` track was completing, and the final owner then redundantly called `container.destroy({children:true})` after manually releasing pooled children; Pixi destruction nulls `_position/_scale`, leaving lazy GSAP property initialization to read `null.x`. Bubble release is now idempotently scheduled on the first safe task after the GSAP frame, Mushroom calls it only after both parallel exit tracks share a timeline end, and the container destroys only itself with `children:false` after every child has been explicitly detached/repooled. A focused contract locks all three lifecycle boundaries and forbids `children:true` from returning. `qa:fast` passes 16 suites / 189 tests plus TypeScript/lint/diff/visual contracts, and the no-native-sync production web build passes. Native Web.bundle/app/phone were not changed; a fresh-reload Mushroom web replay remains `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST` before declaring the runtime crash closed.
- Mushroom growth pile now follows the supplied dense reference more closely: the same fixed pool of 30 Sprites is resized from `100–150px` to `135–200px` and authored into five tightly overlapping front-to-back layers. The first full-width layer owns the highest z-index; every later/higher birth sorts behind it, so the pile visibly grows upward without exposing board paper between stems. The two rear layers narrow toward a centered apex to form the requested inverted-V/pyramid silhouette, with cropped edge slots and center spacing substantially smaller than sprite width. Existing staggered boing birth, reverse boing exit, five shared textures, pollen, timers, pooling, and cleanup remain unchanged. Native Web.bundle/app/phone were not changed; final density and silhouette remain `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST` on web.
- Beach Ball's v800 finale physics were still present but unreachable after the later Juice-profile modularization: both merge entry points changed from the old explicit `variant.id === 'beach-ball'` down-route to `getSpecialDiceJuiceDropProfile(...)`, while the Beach Ball registry definition never received that profile. It therefore entered core Juice's upward branch instead of falling/bouncing. Beach Ball now explicitly owns `juiceDropProfile: 'beach-ball'`; both final-merge starters consequently pass `direction:'down'`, its six ball textures, and the dedicated profile into the unchanged v800 custom-down branch. The restored path is spawn above viewport → accelerated floor fall → `1.26×/0.70×` impact squash → high `power2.out` rebound with lateral travel → below-screen fall/cleanup. A focused two-test contract locks both routing call sites and the complete v800 motion markers separately from Mushroom. `qa:fast` passes 16 suites / 189 tests, type-check/diff/lint/visual contracts pass, the no-native-sync production build/raw-asset restore passes, and release bundle audit passes. - Latest Mushroom crown follow-up supersedes the 25-slot/count statements below. The pile now contains 30 pooled Sprites so each of `mushroom1…5.png` appears exactly six times. The 1024px sources are intentionally used at the existing 100–150px display range; supplied 2048px `@2x` files remain raw assets but are not decoded for this effect, avoiding roughly four-times-per-texture GPU allocation. Every instance is randomized between `100–150px`; edge mushrooms are intentionally cropped, foreground mushrooms are larger/layer above the smaller upper growth, and the first mushrooms remain visible until the complete world has formed. - Honey/Magnet's long post-animation input freeze is now fixed and physically verified end-to-end. Native transaction tracing first proved the original symptom: the Magnet/Honey handler threw before board commit, rollback cleared `magnet-pull` but left the shared `special-transaction` token alive until its 15s watchdog, so every later drag was rejected. All Magnet abort/error/timeout exits now release only their own immutable token after rollback, preventing any exception from freezing the board or a stale callback from clearing a newer transaction. The root exception was then captured exactly as `Can't find variable: usedSpawnLockedTilesWithPop`: `app-merge.ts` retained a dead legacy branch flag even though that spawn path no longer existed. The stale condition is removed and the six reserved placeholders are synchronized unconditionally before board commit; a regression contract forbids that identifier from returning. `qa:full` passes 102 suites / 598 tests plus TypeScript, lint, source/bundle audits, visual contracts, production build, and native guard. The exact output was synced only to Stack to Six, signed, and installed over Wi-Fi on `iPhone 13 blue`; Apple confirmed bundle `com.taptapdesign.stacktosix.Stack-to-Six` at installation URL ending `B91FCA10-C386-4F59-A9EB-26912A475259/Stack to Six.app`. Final physical Honey replay is `PASS`: token 3 claimed at `56887ms`, all `5/5` replacements and board-commit callback confirmed at `58330ms`, exact ownership released at `58331ms`, no merge error or later transaction-blocked drag occurred, and many subsequent drags completed. Representative post-commit drags processed moves around `0.7–1.4ms` with no move over `8ms`; most ticker maxima were `21–32ms`. The preceding Flower/TNT-heavy merge trace contained one isolated `76ms` FX spike, so correctness/unlock is PASS while further Flower finale frame-spike optimization remains separate performance work.
- Special-dice board mutation is now serialized by one reference-free `SpecialDiceTransactionOwner` shared by Star, Juice-family, Magnet-family, and TNT-family dice. Every external special merge claims an immutable monotonically increasing token before the first grid mutation; `canDrop`, drag rebind, and merge entry reject competing transactions, while Magnet's owner-internal pulled-tile merge is explicitly permitted without claiming a second token. Release is exact-token-only, so a delayed callback from an older special cannot clear a newer owner or its input gate. Normal special spawns release from their real async spawn `finally`; the legacy 2.5s spawn timeout cannot release ownership; run/route reset and the independent 15s reference-free watchdog provide abort recovery. Flower/TNT uses a dual barrier at visible Bush completion plus completed bonus replacement spawns. The first physical Honey replay then exposed a separate Magnet timing error: all `5/5` replacements had committed, but the owner remained held through app-merge's fixed `1200ms` endgame settle plus optional `600ms/500ms` retries. Magnet/Honey now signals `onMagnetBoardCommit` only after replacements, consumed-Honey-to-regular conversion, and placeholder synchronization; that callback immediately clears both `magnet-pull` and the exact special token, while later endgame verification continues without freezing input. Consumed Honey identity and tile-local resolution ownership are cleared atomically before the fresh regular value is committed. Focused owner/input/Honey/Magnet tests pass 29/29 and `qa:full` passes 102 suites / 597 tests plus TypeScript, lint, release audits, visual contracts, production build, bundle audit, and native source guard. The complete bundle passed `qa:ios`, signed build, final bundle-ID/content verification, and Wi-Fi install-over on `iPhone 13 blue`; Apple confirmed `com.taptapdesign.stacktosix.Stack-to-Six`, installation URL ending `2FDB5154-0BD9-4C35-B9A0-D2B4E255925C/Stack to Six.app`, and successful launch. Deterministic verdict is `PASS`; immediate post-Honey-board-commit interaction remains `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST` in this exact build.
- Honey's physically reproduced repeat-activation/stuck-tile regression is fixed at both ownership boundaries. The first replay disproved the initial Honey-local fix as sufficient and exposed the upstream race: although Flower/TNT is declared `after-gameplay-resolve`, `tnt-animation.ts` released the all-input gate at the sprite-10 visual lead and again after BOOM-letter exit, exactly while Flower's bonus break/spawn began mutating the board. This allowed a Honey drop to enter an overlapping special transaction. Those visual-tail releases are removed; Flower/TNT now retains input ownership until its sole complete cleanup, so Honey cannot start while any bush/bonus mutation remains. Independently, every gameplay-resolving Honey tile carries a resolution-owner flag rejected by visual-tail rebind, drag-start, gameplay-candidate selection, `canDrop`, and `merge`; magnet conversion atomically clears `_ccWildSpecial`, variant/archetype metadata, and live wild flags before painting the fresh regular cube, invalidating late texture resurrection, and releases ownership only after `setValue` commits. Focused Flower/Honey/input tests pass 23/23; `qa:fast` passes 15 suites / 184 tests. The preceding lifecycle patch passed `qa:full` with 100 suites / 590 tests plus all static gates, and the follow-up passes production build, `qa:ios`, and signed Xcode build. The exact complete output was synced only to Stack to Six and installed over Wi-Fi on `iPhone 13 blue`; Apple confirmed installation URL ending `DEEEA822-38AD-498F-9DDC-C70FCAD4570B/Stack to Six.app`, and that exact bundle launched with live console. The corrected Flower→Honey physical replay remains `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST`.
- Fixed the physically observed frozen regular value-6 when the same merge filled the wild meter at zero moves. The merge callback previously deferred `No Moves` for the pending reward and then returned from the entire merge-6 transaction, skipping its normal replacement spawn and destination cleanup; the locked 6 survived until the 1.2s watchdog while the special reward could begin concurrently. The deferred terminal branch now continues the owning merge-6 cleanup/spawn, while true no-continuation positions still run the fail flow. Wild reward entry is additionally blocked whenever a regular merge-6 destination cleanup claim exists, then remains serialized behind the existing merge spawn guard, so Flower/Honey/Mushroom cannot race the still-owned merge cell. Cleanup ownership now exposes a non-retaining `hasClaim()` query; a modular terminal-handoff decision plus focused behavioral/source-order regressions cover continuation, fail, retry, and ownership ordering. `qa:fast` passes 22 suites / 224 tests and `qa:full` passes 100 suites / 589 tests plus TypeScript, release audit, lint, visual contracts, production build, bundle audit, and native source guard. The exact complete build was synced only into the authoritative Stack to Six `Web.bundle`, passed `qa:ios` before and after signed build, and was installed over Wi-Fi on `iPhone 13 blue`; Apple confirmed installation URL ending `5F66B402-132D-4C49-BFCA-19503D01535B/Stack to Six.app`, and the exact bundle launched with live console. The physical replay captured regular merge-6 `5 + 1`; the board then accepted many subsequent drag/merge operations with no frozen input owner, terminal fail race, or JS error. This confirms the stranded-six lifecycle regression is fixed on-device. The Flower/TNT boundary still produces its known temporary GSAP/timeline workload rise, but it no longer aborts gameplay ownership.
- The complete current build was successfully installed over Wi-Fi on `iPhone 13 blue` after keeping the long-running transfer session alive beyond the initial usage assertion. Apple confirmed `App installed` at installation URL ending `9C4F6E33-9E29-4F8B-B116-322F421CFD09/Stack to Six.app`; the exact bundle `com.taptapdesign.stacktosix.Stack-to-Six` launched in bundled mode with live console. A 30-second Journey Forest Stage 02 trace proved ordinary gameplay is stable: representative one-second windows averaged `16.66–16.67ms`, most worst frames were `17–30ms`, ordinary merges averaged about `15.5–16.5ms`, and drag processing stayed `0.4–2.2ms` with zero moves over `8ms`. Hidden Journey remained only `11 children / 2 images`; no hidden-screen rebuild was present. GSAP work rose during merges but repeatedly returned downward (`592→121→44`, later `767→406→132→76`), so the trace does not show monotonic lifecycle buildup. One real hitch was captured at the special-FX boundary: a one-second window averaged `18.18ms` with a `78ms` worst frame, `2` frames over `34ms`, and `1` over `50ms`; concurrently `will-change` rose `1→10`, GSAP children `76→445`, active timelines `28→94`, and timeouts `5→15`, after which board protection correctly set `reducedFx:true`. The immediately following haptic sequence was heavy then repeated light impacts, which matches the TNT-archetype finale used by Stage 02's first Flower special; this makes Flower/TNT burst creation the strong evidence-based suspect, not Journey idle, touch handling, or ordinary merges. The console tunnel then invalidated, so absolute memory and a second confirming Flower trace remain unavailable. Verdict: `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST`; next work should add/name Flower finale milestones around bush/flower emission and measure the same activation again before optimizing.

On the Journey Worlds Hub, the standard enter remains on each outer World card while the independent nested World/cloud CSS idle owner starts on the first real visible enter tick, so motion is already alive throughout the Forest → Beach → Area 55 cascade without transform contention. On Forest/Beach/Area 55 screens, each Unit finalizes and starts its existing idle immediately when that Unit's own enter tween completes instead of waiting for every later Unit; the coordinator's seamless amplitude ramp is shortened from `0.8s` to `0.18s`. Exit, ordering, enter/exit values, Unit grouping, spatial motion, interaction cleanup, and Reduced Motion behavior are unchanged. It now emits 24 pooled `part1…part6` sprites from a centre-owned horizontal birth zone spanning 50% of the viewport width, rather than one point, with a short 22ms cascade; every part owns one uninterrupted `ease:none` ballistic path, rises/spreads independently, rotates `0.75–1.75` turns, then falls under gravity and is released only below the viewport. Parts remain fully opaque for the visible lifecycle with no bounce, collision solver, wall reflection, fade-out, or direction-change pause. Modern browsers reuse the established zero-safe Homepage→Journey WAAPI owner (`back.in(1.25)` approximation), route only after actual target completion, freeze the currently painted transform when a fast exit interrupts enter, and normalize transforms only after Homepage is hidden; the old CSS/770ms path remains solely as a no-WAAPI fallback. Homepage return enter now exposes and settles a real Promise at final cleanup instead of a separate 1120ms route guess, and Homepage-scoped enter/exit curves use direction-correct `back.out`/`back.in` approximations without crossing through scale zero. Legacy overlay/bottom-sheet `.animate-*` behavior is untouched. Physical animation feel remains `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST`.
- Journey Hub World banners now preserve completed progress history: a World renders its banner when it owns the current interim card **or** when all ten regular cards are unlocked. Completing Forest therefore keeps `10/10` visible while Beach independently shows `0/10`; future Worlds without an interim card and without completion still have no banner. Count calculation, unlock/interim progression, banner motion, and World navigation are unchanged. The focused Hub contract passes (16/16), and `qa:fast` passes (6 suites / 41 tests plus TypeScript, changed-file lint, diff hygiene, and visual contracts). This change is local/web-only and is not installed, committed, or pushed.
- Arcade current-Round entry latency now has an explicit runtime policy. The first local attempt started the cue before `bootGame`; boot cleanup killed that GSAP owner while its Promise remained pending, leaving both cue and cubes blocked. Three independent cold-entry audits then confirmed that first Play uniquely creates the PIXI/WebGL renderer and warms HUD/core textures while later Play reuses them. In native `app://`, the one-shot cue now starts immediately after destructive GSAP cleanup and before cold `Application.init`, covering that work; the user physically confirmed the installed native behavior is good. Desktop `http(s)` does not overlap because synchronous WebGL initialization froze GSAP mid-letter/number; web now starts the same cue immediately after boot and before saved layout/restore, keeping the animation fluid. Fresh/load pop-in consumes the exact same owner before cubes enter, recovery remains post-pop-in, and boot failure explicitly cancels the owner/overlay. Focused tests pass (10/10), and `qa:fast` passes (5 suites / 25 tests plus TypeScript, changed-file lint, diff hygiene, and visual contracts). The native-overlap build passed production sync, `qa:ios`, signing, exact bundle-ID verification, install-over and launch only on `iPhone 13 blue` at installation URL ending `8450E21F-FD92-4B03-BB47-982245631DAE/Stack to Six.app`; existing data was preserved and legacy Kockice Crash was untouched. The subsequent web-only runtime split is local/dev-server-only and is not yet rebuilt into or installed on native, committed, or pushed.

This visual-only cue never emits `Nice/thumb`, advances progression, or replaces the established clean-board transition. `qa:full` passes (92 suites / 559 tests plus type-check, full lint, audits, visual contracts, no-native-sync production build, bundle audit, and native source guard). - Fresh Arcade now shows the same pure current-Round number cue as resume: a brand-new run displays `Round 01` after Homepage exit and before any new cube or ghost can paint, then begins the unchanged `sweetPopIn` for Round 01. It does not reuse clear-phase `Nice/thumb`, does not advance progression, and does not affect the established clean-board Round transition. `startNewGame` arms a one-shot Round 01 cue; `rebuildBoard` captures/clears it, hides the freshly constructed cubes, awaits the shared visual-only cue through the pop-in runner, then releases the original board entrance. The web browser commonly routes Play through saved-state resume even while the save is still Round 01, so the resume gate now accepts every valid saved Round `01+` rather than only `02+`; saved Round 01 therefore receives the same intro before its exact saved cubes enter. Magnet selection and commit previously disagreed about a visible `_ccWildSpawnDropping` target: the caller locked/detached it and marked prospective pull ownership, the handler rejected it, then the caller ignored `false`, skipped rollback/spawn/endgame, and left protected star-flight visuals frozen over an empty logical board. - Arcade Homepage continuation now reads the already-authoritative `cc_arcade_run_state_v1` snapshot and, when its saved `boardNumber`/fallback `level` is Round 02 or higher, presents only the shared pure `Round 0N` number animation as a one-shot resume indicator after the gameplay surface appears. It does not complete a Round, increment progression, reset/rebuild the board, or call `startLevel`; Round 01 keeps the normal entry with no extra cue. Two independent false-advance paths are closed. First, zone teardown previously killed a stage-clear overlay without settling its Promise, then resume cleanup resolved that abandoned owner as `continue`; stage-clear teardown now emits explicit `cancel`, and `endgame-flow` returns without progression. Second, the 1.3s post-load recovery timer previously started while the saved-Round cue still kept every restored tile hidden; it could therefore classify a valid mid-Round save as a clean board and produce ghost exit → Nice/thumb → next Round. Recovery/endgame validation is now scheduled only from the completed saved-board pop-in edge, after the full cue and after tiles are visible, including the pop-in failure fallback. Resume sequencing is therefore deterministic: restore and hide saved cubes → show gameplay/HUD and saved `Round 0N` cue → pop in the exact saved cubes → only then arm recovery validation. The one-shot cue is captured by the load owner and cleared by UI Manager, so it cannot leak into a later load; malformed/missing saves and cue failures fail open into normal resume. Contracts lock pure-visual ownership, cancellation/no-advance behavior, hidden-cubes → cue → cube-pop-in ordering, and recovery ownership after entrance completion. `qa:fast` passes (21 suites / 216 tests plus TypeScript, changed-file lint, diff hygiene, and Journey visual contracts). - Fixed inconsistent physical CTA tap-and-hold on WKWebView. The master controller previously treated `lostpointercapture` as a release, but iOS can emit that event while the finger remains physically down, causing the first hold to rebound to idle while a later hold happened to remain pressed. Capture loss no longer releases the visual; actual `pointerup`/`pointercancel` are observed on both the button and `window`, keyed to the active pointer ID, so a held CTA stays at the pressed scale and an off-element release still settles safely. - CTA migration phase 4 moves all four Homepage slider CTAs (`Play`, `Journey`, `Shop`, and `Settings`) onto the master controller. `UIManager` now registers their original actions as master activations instead of attaching parallel button click handlers; reattachment disposes and recreates controllers safely, while hero-image CTAs remain independent. The Homepage animation owner calls the same controller `prime/enter/exit` lifecycle for startup, normal Homepage transitions, and the dedicated Homepage-to-Journey exit, so legacy root/iPad transforms no longer compete with press/release motion. The master controller now exposes interruption-safe immediate `hidden/idle` priming for screen preparation. Registered Homepage roots drop the legacy `tap-scale/menu-btn-primary` owners, preserve `250px` desktop/iPad and `226px` phone widths, and retain the established phone vertical offset through layout positioning rather than motion transform. Focused CTA contract coverage passes (7/7); `qa:fast` passes (6 suites / 26 tests plus TypeScript, lint, diff hygiene, and visual contracts); and the web-only production build passes with `SKIP_NATIVE_BUNDLE_SYNC=true`. Existing localhost `http://localhost:5174` responds `200`. Native bundle and phone remain untouched; live visual/touch feel is `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST` after web approval.
- Fixed the master CTA exit color flash: `exit()` intentionally disables input immediately, but the generic disabled-state filter also desaturated/darkened the orange visual before its bounce-out, making it look like a brown active overlay. Exit now publishes `data-cta-state="exiting"` before disabling input, and exiting/hidden CTAs explicitly retain their normal color while scaling/fading away. Genuine idle disabled CTAs keep the subdued treatment. Focused CTA coverage passes (6/6), and `qa:fast` passes (6 suites / 25 tests plus TypeScript, lint, diff hygiene, and visual contracts). Web-only; native bundle and phone remain untouched.
- Fixed the Journey detail primary CTA's missing 3D depth: the legacy `#board-detail-play-button { overflow: hidden !important; }` rule was clipping the shared inner visual's `8px` bottom stroke and outer drop shadow. The registered master CTA now explicitly keeps its layout root overflow visible, while `.cc-cta__visual` continues clipping only its own shimmer/content. No dimensions, color, motion, modal lifecycle, or activation logic changed. Focused CTA contract coverage passes (6/6), and `qa:fast` passes (6 suites / 25 tests plus TypeScript, lint, diff hygiene, and visual contracts). Live on localhost web only; native bundle and phone remain untouched.
- CTA migration phase 3 moves both active Journey detail paths onto the master system: regular Stage cards use the floating `Play/Continue` CTA, while interim Stage cards use their embedded `Continue` CTA. Both register the same primary controller, share global enter/press/release/exit timing, and remove the former parallel `click + touchend` activation and homepage CSS animation classes. Detail modal exit invokes the master CTA exit first while preserving the existing card/stats/header choreography; repeated open/close and hard cleanup dispose controllers, floating buttons are removed, and the persistent interim template button is retained hidden for its next clone. Phone detail width remains `220px`, iPad/desktop `249px`, with identical motion. Focused CTA contract coverage passes (6/6), and `qa:fast` passes (6 suites / 25 tests plus TypeScript, lint, diff hygiene, and visual contracts). Live on localhost web only; Homepage CTA, native bundle, and phone are untouched.
- Fixed the first CTA phase-2 visual regression: Tutorial Complete rendered no visible Continue button because its dynamically injected legacy root selector still applied `opacity:0; transform:scale(0)` after the master stylesheet loaded, while the new system correctly animated only `.cc-cta__visual`. The canonical `.cc-cta` root now forcefully owns layout-only invariants (`opacity:1`, no transform/transition), ensuring old screen selectors cannot hide or transform registered roots; all motion remains on the inner visual. This also protects New Reward and Special Dice from the same injected-CSS ordering bug. Contract coverage asserts the ownership boundary and `qa:fast` passes (6 suites / 24 tests plus TypeScript, lint, diff hygiene, and visual contracts). Web-only; no native bundle or phone changed.
- Developer Settings `First Time Run ON` now prepares a genuinely fresh tutorial launch instead of only arming the tutorial flag. Before arming, it removes the global/transient save, completion marker, legacy game state, Arcade resume state, and stale completion runtime flags that could divert the next Homepage Play tap into Continue/resume. Journey progression, board highscores/stars, collectibles, settings, and other player data remain untouched. The reset policy is isolated in `first-play-tutorial-dev-reset.ts` and called before `armFirstPlayTutorial()`. Focused regression tests pass (2/2), and `qa:fast` passes (6 suites / 24 tests plus TypeScript, lint, diff hygiene, and visual contracts). The fix is live on localhost web only; native bundle and phone remain untouched.
- CTA migration phase 2 moves Tutorial Complete, Journey New Reward, and Journey Special Dice `Continue` buttons onto the approved shared CTA system. Each screen now registers one primary CTA, uses the central press-release activation sequence, calls the same global enter/exit profile, and disposes through the existing screen cleanup. Their former legacy class stack, local click listener, and direct GSAP/CSS CTA transform timelines are removed. On exit, the CTA completes its master bounce-out before the existing hero/text/screen exit continues; reward reveal timing still decides when the shared CTA enters. Reward width remains screen-scoped through `.cc-cta-stack--reward`, while motion stays global and identical. Focused contract coverage passes (5/5), and `qa:fast` passes (5 suites / 22 tests plus TypeScript, lint, diff hygiene, and visual contracts). Changes are live on localhost web only; native bundle and phone remain untouched.
- Tutorial Complete (`Congrats! / You cleared the stage`) no longer owns its old `65%` gray overlay plus white radial background recipe. The full-screen overlay now uses the same canonical `applyAppPaperSurfaceToElement()` paper surface as the other gameplay-occluding screens; thumb, copy, layout, CTA, and all existing animations are unchanged. Focused regression coverage passes (1/1), and `qa:fast` passes (5 suites / 20 tests plus TypeScript, lint, diff hygiene, and visual contracts). The change is live on the existing localhost web server; no native bundle or phone was changed.
- CTA-system migration phase 1 is local, web-first, and visually approved for Clean Board plus Board Fail. New `cta-system.ts` is the single DOM motion/input owner for registered CTA buttons, with primary/secondary roles, interruption-safe GSAP enter/exit/press/release, Pointer Events, double-activation protection, disabled state, and explicit disposal. `CTA_MOTION_DEFAULTS`, mutable `ctaMotion`, `configureCtaMotion()`, and `resetCtaMotion()` provide one global tuning surface for enter, exit, press, release, easing, press scale, and companion stagger. `exitCtaPair()` owns mirrored clicked-first pair exit ordering, so screens no longer carry their own stagger values. Activation finishes the complete press-release bounce before invoking the action; the clicked CTA then exits into itself first and its companion follows `70ms` later. New scoped `cta-system.css` isolates the animated inner visual from screen layout transforms and defines separate phone (`<=767px`) and iPad (`768–1024px`) width tokens while sharing identical motion. Clean Board and Board Fail legacy class collisions and parallel touch/mouse transform handlers are removed without changing their actions or surrounding modal choreography. Focused contract tests pass (3/3), and `qa:fast` passes (5 suites / 20 tests plus TypeScript, lint, diff hygiene, and visual contracts). The existing dev server at `http://localhost:5174` is serving the source for review. No native bundle or phone was changed; physical feel remains `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST` after the full migration is approved on web.
- Checkpoint `v906` consolidates all accepted work after `v905`: the gentler session-stable independent Journey gyro profiles, correct forced `Try It` activation, per-World ten-Stage difficulty and star thresholds, transient-safe wild/preload-meter credit, and canonical New Reward paper background. - Journey `New Reward / Tap the card to reveal` no longer owns a separate `65%` gray paper overlay and flat fallback. Its full-screen overlay now calls the canonical `applyAppPaperSurfaceToElement()` helper used by the other gameplay-occluding screens, so paper texture, warm gradient, opacity, positioning, and future global paper changes remain identical. A source-contract regression test prevents the local recipe from returning. The focused test passes (1/1) and `qa:fast` passes (10 suites / 101 tests plus TypeScript, lint, diff hygiene, and visual contracts). - Fixed a wild/preload-meter race reproduced near Journey endgame: a non-final Juice/Star/magnet merge could be correctly classified from its immutable merge-entry snapshot, then `addWildProgress()` would re-check the transient live board while a backpack wild was still hidden/dropping or before merge-6 replacement tiles spawned. That second check could falsely see a last pair, reset the meter, and discard the earned merge charge. Meter credit now consumes the existing confirmed-non-final decision for regular stack merges, non-final merge-6, and validated magnet pulls; true final merges still reset/skip charge, and unrelated blockers (`busyEnding`, disabled meter, transitions) remain intact. The decision lives in `wild-meter-progress-decision.ts` with focused regression coverage. Targeted wild/final/magnet suites pass (5 suites / 32 tests), `qa:fast` passes (9 suites / 100 tests), and `qa:full` passes (80 suites / 525 tests plus TypeScript, lint, production build without native sync, bundle audits, visual contracts, and native source guard). The exact fix is included in the installed/launched Stack to Six build recorded above. Changes remain uncommitted/unpushed; physical reproduction remains `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST`.
- Journey gameplay difficulty and completion stars now share one per-World ten-Stage balance contract. Forest `1–10`, Beach `11–20`, and Area 55 `21–30` each reset to the same arc: local Stages `1–3` are very easy (`75%` small-value assistance), `4–6` medium (`40%`), and `7–10` use the normal unassisted `1–5` pool. Journey star thresholds follow those same tiers: `2★/3★` at `2500/6500`, `3000/8000`, and `3500/9500`. Completion modal calculation, saved-high-score World stars, initial tiles, and later regular spawns consume the same modular source; Arcade and tutorial balance are unchanged. Focused balance/spawn tests pass (26/26), `qa:fast` passes (8 suites / 96 tests), and `qa:full` passes (79 suites / 521 tests plus TypeScript, lint, production build without native sync, bundle audits, visual contracts, and native source guard). The exact `dist` and complete raw assets were synced only to the authoritative Stack to Six `Web.bundle`; `qa:ios`, signed Xcode build, final `com.taptapdesign.stacktosix.Stack-to-Six` bundle-ID/content verification, and Wi-Fi install-over passed. Apple confirmed installation URL ending `9F7B71E5-2A47-4B39-BEBD-FAA74D120B51/Stack to Six.app`, and that exact bundle launched successfully on `iPhone 13 blue`. No fallback device or legacy shell was touched. Changes remain uncommitted/unpushed; gameplay feel remains `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST`.

Journey Hub, Homepage, gameplay, detail modal, Round Complete, board transition, sensor range, and maximum low-level strength are unchanged. `qa:fast` passes (6 suites / 70 tests), production build, `qa:ios`, and signed Stack to Six build pass. - The accepted follow-up direction removes separate acceleration/return timing: every Journey scene now uses one symmetric `270ms` elapsed-time response in both directions for a single gentle follow. Each fresh Journey Hub entry samples three small stable offsets—World path rotation, World depth rotation, and cloud direction rotation—then keeps that profile frozen for the complete Hub visit. Forest/Beach/Area 55 therefore receive different path assignments on later entries while preserving their inward side, and all seven clouds receive a newly rotated set of independent directions/depths. Re-activation on the same mounted Hub reuses the profile; there is no per-frame or per-tilt RNG, new listener, timer, RAF, or cleanup owner. `qa:fast` passes (6 suites / 70 tests), production build, `qa:ios`, and signed Stack to Six build pass. - Physical review of the first truly independent Hub build confirmed separation but found its motion jerky and unnatural. Journey smoothing is now frame-rate independent: the single existing RAF uses elapsed-time exponential response (`150ms` intentional pickup, `225ms` return) rather than a fixed `0.145/0.09` per-frame fraction, so 60/120Hz and isolated iOS frame variation produce the same feel. Hub inward motion removes the noisy radial cusp with a continuous soft-centre magnitude and rebalances from `72/28` to `62%` inward pull / `38%` directional per-World wander, preserving individual trajectories and centre bias while reducing rectified in/out pumping. No listener, RAF owner, timer, or target count was added. `qa:fast` passes (6 suites / 69 tests), production build, `qa:ios`, and signed Stack to Six build pass. No fallback device or legacy shell was touched. This refinement remains uncommitted and unpushed; physical motion feel remains `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST`.
- `Try It` on a Developer-forced Tilt Motion intro previously failed whenever the saved 3D toggle was OFF: the modal bypassed the visibility policy correctly, but its callback called the controller while `isEnabled()` was still false, so permission returned immediately and the modal nevertheless resolved as `enabled`. The launch CTA now temporarily enables the runtime setting before the synchronous gesture-owned Apple permission request, persists ON only after a granted result, and restores/persists the previous state on denial or failure. The modal now resolves failed requests as `cancelled`, never `enabled`. `qa:fast` passes (6 suites / 68 tests), production build, `qa:ios`, signed Stack to Six build, exact bundle ID, and matching entrypoints pass. After restarting only Mac `CoreDeviceService`, Wi-Fi install-over finally succeeded on `iPhone 13 blue` at installation URL ending `40DE8A0E-9328-4D14-BEEA-C42291EF092D/Stack to Six.app`, and the exact bundle launched successfully. This installed build includes the Try It correction, three independent Hub World pitch/roll matrices with inward bias, and seven independent Hub cloud directions. No fallback device or legacy shell was touched. The changes remain uncommitted and unpushed; physical motion feel remains `NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST`.
- The first inward-Hub refinement still felt grouped because all three Worlds consumed the same mixed Hub tilt before only depth/polarity differences were applied. Each Hub World now owns a distinct pitch/roll mixing matrix: Forest, Beach, and Area 55 derive different X/Y trajectories from the same physical gesture. Horizontal travel is composed from a `72%` magnitude-driven inward pull plus `28%` per-World directional wander, so Forest/Area 55 independently move right and Beach independently moves left toward the screen centre as tilt grows; releasing the phone returns each along its own path. This supersedes the preceding symmetric `42%` outward attenuation. Cloud independence remains unchanged. Focused tests pass (31/31), `qa:fast` passes (4 suites / 47 tests), production build, `qa:ios`, and signed Stack to Six build pass. The latest install-over attempt was again interrupted by Apple's `IXRemoteErrorDomain code 6`, so this exact correction is not installed, committed, or pushed. No fallback device or legacy shell was touched.
- Physical review of the first sensitive-centre prototype accepted the stronger small-tilt response but rejected the Hub's rigid two-plane motion. Hub Worlds now retain separate session-stable depth/vertical directions while their horizontal polarity points Forest and Area 55 rightward and Beach leftward toward the composition centre; reverse/outward travel remains visible at `42%` amplitude so the islands breathe independently without flying apart. Every Hub cloud now receives its own session-stable two-axis direction plus existing distinct depth, producing seven independent cloud paths without per-frame randomness, new listeners, timers, or RAF owners. Focused tests pass (30/30), `qa:fast` passes (4 suites / 46 tests), production build and `qa:ios` pass, and the signed app is verified as `com.taptapdesign.stacktosix.Stack-to-Six` with matching entrypoints. No fallback device or legacy shell was touched.
- Latest local-only Journey gyro tuning replaces the weak linear centre response with a Journey-scene-only ease-out curve: relaxed wrist motion is amplified most near centre while the existing maximum remains unchanged. Hub Worlds now share one movement polarity instead of session-random opposing directions, preserving modest depth variation while preventing the three islands from visually pulling apart; Hub clouds keep their authored counter-depth. - Player-facing progression terminology is now consistent across the app: every Journey board is presented as **Stage / Stages**, and every Arcade stage is presented as **Round / Rounds**. `gameplay-terminology.ts` is the single source for dynamic labels used by the HUD, fail/clear/exit flows, and Arcade round transition; Journey World ranges, detail fallbacks, tutorial copy, achievements, settings/dev controls, navigation accessibility labels, and resume copy were updated too. Internal `board` save/state/API names and Pixi `stage` names remain unchanged, preserving storage and gameplay compatibility. Focused terminology tests, targeted ESLint, type-check, `git diff --check`, and the no-sync production build pass. This change is installed on `iPhone 13 blue` in the latest bundled build.
- Regular gameplay cubes now share one controllable cartoon motion family across Journey and Arcade. New `gameplay-tile-cartoon-motion.ts` centrally owns separate `stack.strength` and `idle.strength` plus bounded timings/poses. Every regular stack contact randomly chooses a visible-but-limited vertical stretch or horizontal squash cycle (maximum about 10%) with anticipation, rebound, and exact `1,1` settle; the previous uniform-only scale pulse is removed. After the existing four-second no-input delay, the idle owner uses the same random variants at a gentler maximum of about 8.5%, with a small tilt and the existing one-tile-at-a-time cadence/smoke policy. Both timelines remain AnimationManager-owned, replacement-safe, and cleanup-safe; merge decisions, wild/merge-6 FX, smoke/shards, scoring, and input timing are unchanged. Five focused motion/interim tests, targeted ESLint, type-check, `git diff --check`, and no-sync production build pass. This motion profile is installed on `iPhone 13 blue` in the latest bundled build.
- Journey board gameplay's randomized `bottom1–12.png` foreground no longer begins its enter while the board transition can still cover it or while the selected DOM image is undecoded. `startLevel()` now only primes/decodes the foreground at `y:84`, lightly vertically squashed and transparent; the existing visible HUD/board-enter callback owns one tracked `0.62s` `back.out(1.9)` GSAP pop-in to its final position. Repeated HUD callbacks are idempotent, hide/exit invalidates pending decode work through a lifecycle token, active enter/exit tweens are killed/replaced cleanly, and the old layout-driving `bottom` CSS transition was removed. This applies to all 12 Journey bottom variants. The Journey bottom-decor scope test (3 tests), targeted ESLint, type-check, `git diff --check`, and no-sync production build pass. It is installed on `iPhone 13 blue` in the latest bundled build.
- Paper-background ownership is now centralized and visually consistent. `body` is the one visible viewport paper owner through first frame, TapTap preload, Homepage, Journey, and Settings; the launch container and legacy `#global-bg` are transparent, and the launch module's retired multi-owner `setBackground()` branch was removed. Fail Board, Clear Board, and the short pre-Clear handoff cover still correctly occlude the live gameplay canvas, but all three now render through the same canonical shared helper instead of their darker 65% paper recipes. Targeted ESLint, `npm run type-check`, `git diff --check`, and `SKIP_NATIVE_BUNDLE_SYNC=true npm run build` pass. This paper-owner change is installed on `iPhone 13 blue` in the latest bundled build.
- Journey World image readiness can no longer strand a screen at `opacity: 0`: the already-complete `img.decode()` branch now shares the same hard deadline as load/error listeners instead of returning an unbounded promise. Live Hub → World and homepage-visible World enters do not block their local/cached Unit animation on all decorative image decodes. This addresses the web trace where preparation logged but `world-enter-images-ready`/complete never followed.
- A manual Journey Hub → World tap is now the authoritative start of a fresh visible lifecycle: it clears stale tutorial/game-return suppression markers, and `applyJourneyV700WorldScope` never lets those old flags override the live `__ccJourneyV700Opening` owner. Background and real return renders still prime safely, but a user-selected Forest/Beach/Area 55 can no longer be left hidden by residue from an earlier flow.
- Every current and future Journey Worlds Hub item now uses the selected World card as a first-class target inside the shared Hub exit timeline. It matches the active cjelina-card motion with one continuous visible scale-to-zero over `0.40s` using `back.in(1.7)`; that easing supplies the single initial outward nudge before the World folds into itself. There is no separate `back.out` punch to duplicate the outward lobe, and opacity is removed only after shrinking finishes. Other Worlds, clouds, and nav continue their coordinated standard exit with interruption-safe completion.
- Product-facing Journey world 3 terminology is now **Area 55** everywhere: Hub/header/accessibility metadata and the former `ROBO MAIN` / `ROBOT RIFT` board labels. Existing internal `robo-*` CSS classes, asset paths, and identifiers remain stable implementation details and are not user-visible.
- Journey Worlds Forest now has an additional upper-left cloud explicitly owned by world 1. The complete Journey map canvas—paper, Hub/world content, and clouds—uses zero inherited horizontal content padding, so both sides reach the physical viewport edge and share one clip boundary; header controls retain their own safe-area padding. The Hub's vertical extent and compact Area 55 bottom trim are unchanged.
- Journey visible-enter ownership now distinguishes a live Hub → World tap from a remembered World rendered during homepage background preparation. Background rendering only primes World Units; the visible Journey lifecycle starts their enter once the screen is revealed, preventing Forest from remaining at `opacity: 0` when a stored Forest view is restored on web/iOS.
- The installed v813 follow-up is currently local-only: board intro haptics are packed into six compact grouped `light` beats (45ms minimum spacing) instead of four, still sampled from the actual pop-in wave and lifecycle-owned. Regular merge-6 keeps the same capped pooled particle count and wide/upward travel, but now uses a spatial smoke-cloud alpha profile: opaque/randomized central puffs, progressively softer side puffs, and a short natural fade rather than uniformly solid instant removal.
- Checkpoint `v813 polish Journey idle, merge smoke and grouped board haptics` is online at commit `1d22596`. Its complete 717-file raw asset tree was synced into the bundled-mode Stack to Six shell, the final app verified as `com.taptapdesign.stacktosix.Stack-to-Six`, and install-over succeeded on `iPhone 13 blue` at installation URL ending `A965C28C-8CC0-4167-8A5F-98BB7255EAFC/Stack to Six.app`. The exact bundle launched successfully after installation.
- Board intro haptics are restored as four grouped `light` beats sampled from the actual randomized cube-entry wave, not one native bridge call per/every-other tile. They run only while the real board enter flag owns the pop-in, are tracked with the same delayed-call cleanup as the animation, preserve load/resume suppression, and have focused scheduler coverage.
- Interim landing dust is now 40% more opaque than the prior range, capped at solid white, while keeping the same bounded particle count and replace-on-each-landing cleanup. Regular merge-6 smoke now sells a broken-cube impact through larger solid-white pooled puffs, wider/random outward travel, and an upward bias. Its particle count is explicitly capped below the previous default multiplier, so the stronger visual does not add density/FPS load. The old board-intro per-tile haptics were confirmed removed in v800 when pop-in moved to the centralized frame-budget scheduler; the new grouped cadence above replaces that retired implementation.
- The interim Board card idle was retuned after visual review: it now uses a short five-stage cartoon anticipation, airborne jump, landing, rebound, and clean settle instead of the overly weak 1.045 sine lift. Each repeat safely chooses either a vertical stretch-jump or horizontal squash-pop pose inside the same single owned GSAP timeline. Every completed landing now replaces the prior smoke container with one bounded fresh puff, giving consistent smoke without overlap buildup. Its light treatment mirrors the TapTap launch-logo masked sweep; a strong white-hot/gold screen-blend core lights the question mark while a separate wrapper-owned halo expands outside the clipped card. Neither layer restores the old interval/reflow/filter restart path.
- Journey world idle ownership was rebuilt after a physical-iPhone report that Forest main, Units, and the Board 04 interim card progressively became jerky during a several-minute idle soak. All Forest/Beach/Area 55 Unit motion now shares one GSAP frame ticker instead of one ticker per Unit/cloud, and a single IntersectionObserver pauses transform updates and releases `will-change` for Units more than 240px outside the Journey viewport. Re-entering Units ramp from their current transform to avoid a visibility-edge snap.
- The interim card no longer runs the old 16% scale / 2.5-degree / 240ms punch or emits 25-35 DOM smoke particles roughly every 640ms. Its policy is now a gentle 4.5% scale, 0.7-degree, 3.5px lift with a 0.62s rise and 0.78s settle; smoke is non-overlapping, reduced, and rate-limited to at most once per 5.2s. Smoke absence no longer marks a healthy infinite bounce timeline stale and repeatedly restarts it.
- Interim shimmer/glow ownership is now one persistent `interim-idle-effects-active` CSS lifecycle class. The old 2.9s interval, forced layout reads, nested RAF restarts, filter/drop-shadow animation, stale timeout fields, and retired CSS classes were removed. Render no longer starts interim idle before visible world enter completes. Infinite interim timelines are explicitly removed from `AnimationManager` ownership when killed.
- Focused regression tests cover gentle interim motion/smoke cadence and explicit external-timeline release. Three targeted suites pass (14 tests), targeted ESLint passes, `npm run type-check` passes, and `SKIP_NATIVE_BUNDLE_SYNC=true npm run build` passes. This idle refactor is installed in the latest bundled build but still needs a physical multi-minute soak test on `iPhone 13 blue`.
- A hotspot-era device discovery attempt failed before listing devices because Apple's CoreDeviceService timed out during initialization; no device, native bundle, or app was touched. Vite is running locally on `http://localhost:5174/` (hotspot interface `172.20.10.2`) for browser inspection only; Stack to Six remains bundled-mode and was not pointed at the dev server.
- Journey Worlds hub scroll ownership now follows the updated product decision: every hub render, including `return-from-world`, clears the retired saved hub position and starts at `scrollTop = 0`. The old save/restore property, helper, calls, storage behavior, and stale test expectation were removed; individual Forest/Beach/Area 55 interim-card auto-scroll remains unchanged. A fixed sync/RAF/layout reset alone was physically insufficient because iOS/layout mutated the scroller after that window, so the hub now rejects programmatic scroll until the user's first real pointer/touch/wheel intent, then removes its listeners and gives manual scrolling full ownership.
- Journey world return phase one now reuses the exact pre-reveal preparation instead of repeating `killTweensOf`/`gsap.set` across all 88 Forest targets. `JourneyWorldAnimationCoordinator` owns one finalization pass, and idle begins on the next animation frame with an explicit cancellation path; the v701 duration, easing, scale, position, Unit order, and irregular offsets are unchanged.
- A narrow iOS-only `world-enter-performance` audit now measures the actual game-to-world return window and reports frame count, average, worst frame, and threshold counts. Its summary utility has focused tests. All 35 Journey-focused tests, targeted ESLint, type-check, and the production build passed.
- Arcade wild-star orbit suppression is now owned by `wild-stars.ts`, not only its FX wrapper: attach, async texture/fallback completion, and the shared ticker all reject or clean orbit halos whenever the run mode is Arcade. Journey orbit halos remain enabled, and `run-mode.test.ts` covers the policy.
- Journey Worlds now applies the extra bottom trim to the hub's actual `min-height` owner as well as the cloud layer, and clips cloud overflow at that boundary, removing the residual scroll tail below Area 55 without moving any world.
- The merge multiplier badge now always uses the original brown/taupe disk with light text. The prior intentional white-over-Wild branch was the source of the inconsistent white/brown appearance and has been removed.
- Validation for these three fixes passed: targeted `run-mode.test.ts` (2 tests), targeted ESLint, `npm run type-check`, and `SKIP_NATIVE_BUNDLE_SYNC=true npm run build`.
- Physical installs and tests must always target `iPhone 13 blue`; other visible iPhones and iPads are not fallback targets unless the user explicitly changes this rule.
- Journey Worlds Forest Unit now includes three added framing clouds: one large upper-left, one large upper-right, and one medium at Forest's lower-right edge. Screenshot-guided tuning moved both right-side clouds another 60px left so their visible cloud mass sits close to the Forest island while preserving their diagonal vertical separation. All three are explicitly owned by world 1 so lock, enter/exit, and idle state stay with Forest.
- Journey Worlds hub first trims the paper/scroll tail below Area 55 by 8% of the stable viewport height, then removes another 40% of the measured remaining tail from the absolute cloud-layer box. The cloud layer was the real scrollHeight blocker; world positions, inter-world spacing, visible clouds, and Journey motion remain unchanged.
- Arcade gameplay must not show wild-star orbiting idle decoration; ownership now lives in `wild-stars.ts` across attach, async completion, and ticker cleanup.
- Journey orbiting stars remain enabled.
- Wild-star merge/reward flow remains connected.
- `npm run type-check` and `SKIP_NATIVE_BUNDLE_SYNC=true npm run build` passed after this change.
- This Arcade-only change is included in the latest bundled Stack to Six install.
- The repository now has an automatic continuity layer: `AGENTS.md` requires every new chat to read `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md` and this handoff before acting.
- Global skills `stack-to-six-project-context` and `cube-crash-dev-production-modes` now identify Stack to Six as the only normal iOS target and treat Kockice Crash as explicitly opt-in legacy infrastructure.
- Arcade first-play tutorial HUD cleanup now separates the active tutorial lock from the post-tutorial completion assist. Close/help controls restore immediately after step 4 and no longer remain disabled when entering Journey before an app restart.
- Regression coverage: `first-play-tutorial-hud-lock.test.ts` passes both active-tutorial and completion-assist-only states. Targeted ESLint, `npm run type-check`, and a no-sync production build also pass.
- The current web build, including centralized Arcade orbit cleanup, the corrected Journey Worlds bottom extent, the always-brown merge multiplier, the latest Forest cloud placement, and tutorial HUD lifecycle fix, has been synced into the Stack to Six `Web.bundle` and built successfully as `/tmp/stack-to-six-derived/Build/Products/Debug-iphoneos/Stack to Six.app`.

## Latest physical-iPhone state

- After the 2026-08-20 physical trace, TNT/Flower input ownership was narrowed to the minimum safe surface. TNT now triggers its board-return/bonus selection at frame 6 instead of the later frame-10 exit lead; once blast displacement is restored it reserves only the exact up-to-four bonus tiles through `tnt-bonus-tile-ownership.ts`, releases the global `tnt-boom` lock, and moves the immutable special transaction to wild-only visual-tail scope. Stable ordinary sub-six stacks on every other tile can continue while the reserved tiles finish their staggered removal/replacement; reserved tiles, sum-six transactions, and specials remain serialized. Exact ownership is cleared on removal, completion, empty/final branches, and error fallback. The final special-merge path also releases its exact Star/Juice/Magnet/TNT token after final visual handoff and before clean-board modal ownership, removing the Star-token stale reset observed in the physical trace. Focused regression coverage passes 6 suites / 33 tests; `qa:fast` passes 40 suites / 371 tests plus TypeScript, changed lint, diff hygiene, and visual contracts; the no-sync production build succeeds with 932 modules. `http://127.0.0.1:5174` is serving markers `sprite-6-enter-complete`, `bonus-targets-reserved`, `tnt-board-commit`, and `final-merge-clean-handoff`. Source and `dist` changed; Stack to Six `Web.bundle`, signed app, and phone were not updated. Verdict: **NEEDS PHYSICAL TEST** after explicit web approval.
- On 2026-08-20 the complete current dirty-tree build was approved on `http://localhost:5174`, rebuilt in bundled production mode (931 modules), restored with the full raw asset tree, synced only to the authoritative Stack to Six `Web.bundle`, passed `qa:ios`, and built successfully as a signed Debug iPhone app. The final app was verified as `com.taptapdesign.stacktosix.Stack-to-Six`, bundled mode (`useDevServer=false`), with matching `dist`/source/final-app `index.html`, `assets/tile.png`, and `PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy`. Install-over preserved data and succeeded on exact `iPhone 13 blue` at installation URL ending `5FDED671-D259-4022-ACD6-9C54D7F1FA4C/Stack to Six.app`; no other device or legacy shell was touched.
- The subsequent user-directed physical trace ran for about 415 seconds and is preserved at `/private/tmp/stack-to-six-live-20260820-0022.log`. It contains no JS console errors, warnings, uncaught exceptions, or error-boundary events. Repeated Journey Hub/World returns reached `display:flex`, `visibility:visible`, `opacity:1`; all three World images reported `journey-v700-world-idle` running with changing transform matrices, so the intermittent static/signless Worlds failure did not reproduce. The final Hub return completed in 733ms. A five-second card-overlay performance window averaged 17.27ms with one isolated 128ms worst frame; gameplay drag handlers stayed below 8ms in every observed sample and showed no sustained frame pressure.
- The same trace physically validates the new Magnet visual-tail handoff: token 8 reached `magnet-board-commit-ready`, switched to `board-committed-visual-tail`, accepted an ordinary stack immediately, detected the newer board revision at `magnet-post-commit-tail-superseded`, and released the exact Magnet token. Multiple Juice, TNT, Star, and Magnet transactions otherwise claimed/released cleanly; the previously observed Juice-to-regular-2/false-NO-MOVES race did not reproduce. Eight drag blocks were recorded: five ordinary-tile attempts during TNT gameplay ownership (about 2.3-2.9s per transaction), two special-on-special attempts blocked by Juice bubbles, and one ordinary attempt after `terminal-no-moves` had already committed. These are classified locks, not unexplained stack rejection. One diagnostic anomaly remains: during Play Again a still-active Star token 13 received a null-token reset request (`release-stale-token`) before the following new-run reset showed no active owner. It caused no visible freeze or stale mutation in this run, but should receive a focused cleanup-token audit before App Store sign-off.
- Two Journey board completions were observed without a console error. The trace channel did not emit `CC_FINAL_RESIDUAL_OWNER`, so it cannot by itself prove or disprove the user's intermittent duplicate ghost-placeholder pop-out; that issue still needs a dedicated physical reproduction with the residual-owner diagnostic enabled/verified.

## Current UX decisions worth preserving

- The startup background is one continuous shared paper surface; no white/gray interstitial frame.
- Intro sequence uses `taplogo.png` plus one random `lik-*` character, not the retired combined preload art or a separate Stack to Six logo phase.
- Journey Worlds hub and individual Journey world scroll behavior must remain distinct as documented in `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Journey enter/exit/nav motion must follow `JOURNEY_ANIMATION_CONTRACT.md`.
- Arcade gameplay must not show orbiting wild-star idle decorations; Journey still may.
- Post-tutorial completion assist may stay active until the tutorial board ends, but it must never keep HUD close/help navigation locked.

## How to maintain this file

Update only concrete continuation state: branch/checkpoint, meaningful uncommitted files, latest validated behavior, and what is or is not installed. Move lasting architecture or workflow facts to `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
